   9|                                                                             Vocational. Projects In Your Community
  10|                           Unlike students in traditional, comprehensive high schools, students at Monty Tech are asked to put their education
  11|                           into practice on a daily basis. Students across the twenty different vocational technical education programs are
  12|                           building homes, reconstructing damaged properties, repairing service vehicles, making walkways more accessible, and
  13|                           performing countless community services.
  14|                           The 2010-2011 school year was a busy one for our vocational educators, as each trade aimed to provide practical,
  15|                           hands-on learning experiences for our students, while helping to improve each of the eighteen member communities.
  16|                           Auto Body: Collision Repair Technology: During 2010-2011, the program had eight students participate in SkillsUSA, all of whom finished in the top ten of their competition. There were two seniors accepted into the co-operative educational
  17|                           program, completing their work at local collision shops. The shop continued to repair many vehicles and equipment
  18|                           for staff, the school and district communities. The shop is pleased to have a new spray booth being installed,
  19|                           providing students with training in more efficient water borne paint systems. The shop is in the process of re-certifying
  20|                           NATEF certification. (Total enrollment: 59; 40 males, 19 females)
  21|                           Automotive Technology: Two of our top students were asked to compete at the state level in the Mass Tech
  22|                           Competition. Alex Gay and Jonathan Erickson (Class of 2011) represented Monty Tech as Team Audi. Four students
  23|                           attended the SkillsUSA district competition. Alex Gay, Jonathan Erickson, Robin Murray and Daniel Mugure (Class of
  24|                           2011). The automotive program was fortunate to receive numerous equipment upgrades throughout the year, including:
  25|                           a new Coates tire changer, new shop benches with drawers for the freshmen tools, a new Brannik strut compressor,
  26|                           and a new fluid exchange machine. An updated scan tool was added to the new Snap On Verdict, providing a new
  27|                           graphing system operation and wireless communication capabilities, and the Hunter alignment machine was updated
  28|                           to be compatible with the new electronic steering systems and wireless communication. (Total enrollment: 63; 54
  29|                          males, 9 females)
  30|                           Business Technology: The Business Technology program has been enhanced by the addition of new software and
  31|                          technology, a new exploratory process and curriculum, and an increased presence in the school community. Students
  32|                           and instructors in the Business Technology program assist shops and offices throughout the school by coordinating
  33|                          bulk mailing, labeling, and organizing collating jobs. Offices that receive direct support form the Business Technology
  34|                           program include: Monty Tech Nurses Office, front office, and Student Support Services. Students also assist wherever
  35|                          there is a customer service need, as in the annual greenhouse project, directed by Mr. Dylan Hagar. Students serve as
  36|                           customer service representatives and cashiers for this very busy seasonal operation. Students also run a successful
  37|                          school store, refining their skills in cash handling, customer service, organization, and marketing. During 2010-2011,
  38|                          the shop received new software programs that have had a direct impact on the students' employability: WinWay
  39|                          Resume Deluxe and QuickBooks Pro 2011. (Total enrollment: 60; 11 males, 49 females)
  40|                          Cabinetmaking:        Cabinetmaking students spent a great deal of time building and installing over forty custom
  41|                          bookcases in the Barre children's library, a project with an estimated value exceeding $40,000. Students also made and
  42|                          installed all of the architectural millwork inside the Stone House at Fitchburg's Coggshall Park, made over eighty feet of
  43|                          railings for the outside of the Stone House, crafted numerous gift sets for the Annual Monty Tech Golf Tournament and
  44|                          the Annual Superintendent's Dinner, and built and installed custom solid cherry kitchen cabinets, vanities, and solid
  45|                          surface counters in the house built by Monty Tech students (located in Gardner). Five student projects traveled from
  46|                          Monty Tech to the annual New England Student Wood Design Competition, held at Marlborough High School. There
  47|                          were forty-six student projects submitted from all over New England and Monty Tech students placed 12th, 5th, 3rd, and
  48|                          1st. First place went to Leighton Noel from Barre (Class of 2012), who entered a solid cherry gun cabinet, and received
  49|                          the top award $500. Finally, the program also purchased an OMGA cross cut saw and a new Powermatic wood lathe,
  50|                          and is training students to use this new technology safely and efficiently. (Total enrollment: 71; 38 males, 33 females)
  51|                          Cosmetology: Over the course of the 2010-2011 school year, twelve Seniors took and passed the Massachusetts State
  52|                          Board exam. The program continues to run a full-service clinic, servicing many outside clients as well as staff and
  53|                          students that walk-in. Two cosmetology students qualified for the SkillsUSA State competition. Allison Soucy (Class
  54|                          of 2011) placed 2nd, and was awarded a silver medal, selected from over five hundred entrants, and Kaitlyn Thibault
  55|                                                                                                   46

===PAGE 50=== (ocr, 31 lines)

  37|                    During the 2010-2011 school year, the Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District provided special
  38|                    education services to over two hundred students. In September 2010, the Student Support Services Department was actively providing services to approximately three hundred students identified with disabilities, both on 504 plans and
  39|                   Individual Education Programs (lEP'S).
  40|                   The Student Support Services Department at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School provides support to
  41|                    all students. It has full-time nursing staff that administer medications, perform state mandated health screening exams,
  42|                    and provide, when necessary, health information to the TEAM for a student's IEP meeting. The department employs a
  43|                    full-time school social worker that is able to participate in TEAM meetings and assist students who have needs
  44|                    concerning finances, family issues, homelessness, maternity, health issues, and proper food and clothing. Our students have access to the services of a full-time adjustment counselor, part-time school psychologist and school
  45|                    social worker. All of these individuals are available for scheduled counseling sessions and mental health emergency
  46|                    treatment, as well as crisis intervention. We have on staff a full-time psychologist, whose role it is to evaluate all
  47|                    students referred for an initial evaluation or who require a three-year re-evaluation. In addition, we have a full-time
  48|                    speech pathologist who is available to assist students with disabilities, assess these students and consult with
  49|                    The Special Education Program at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District is reviewed annually in
  50|                    May, in accordance with regulatory requirements. The results of this evaluation are used to improve the special
  51|                    education procedures and programs at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School. The Parent Advisory
  52|                    Council participates in this review and evaluation.

  26|                     Consequently, participating schools were required to enroll fewer students this year. However, this did not adversely

   1|                    TOWN MEETING EXCERPTS:
   2|                    ANNUAL TOWN MEETING MAY 7, 2011
   3|                    The 2011 Annual Town Meeting was called to order in the Lunenburg High School Auditorium at 9:05 A.M. by Moderator
   4|                    Timothy W. Murphy, Esq. a quorum having been declared present. The Town Clerk noted that the warrant for the
   5|                    meeting was properly served. The pledge of allegiance was recited and we thanked eight retirees for their combined
   6|                    one hundred and ninety nine years of service to the town. At 9:10 the Annual Town Meeting was recessed in order to

  38|                             Use and replace with a new Section 4.6.5. Design Standards, as follows:
  39|                            4.6.5. DESIGN STANDARDS
  40|                            4.6.5.1. Purposes. The purposes of this Section are:
  41|                            a) To assure development which is compatible with prevailing architecture and character of the Town.
  42|                            b) To protect the environment.
  43|                            c) To enhance rather than detract from the quality and character of the Town.
  44|                             d) To preserve and enhance property values.
  45|                            4.6.5.2. Standards. In addition to requirements contained elsewhere in this Bylaw the following design elements shall
  46|                            pertain in the Commercial District and, where applicable, shall be reviewed and approved by the Planning Board in
  47|                            connection with Development Plan Review under Section 8.4.

   1|                  a) Occupied Lot Area
   2|                         The gross floor area of all buildings and structures on a lot shall occupy not more than forty (40) percent of the total lot area.
   3|                  2. The total area on any lot devoted to building, parking, outdoor storage and display, and other paved hard surfaced
   4|                  b) Building Location and Utilities. areas may occupy up to eighty-five (85%) percent of the total lot area.
   5|                         The Building front shall face the street on which the lot obtains its frontage unless otherwise approved by the
   6|                  2. If there is more than one building on the site, the siting shall be approved by the Planning Board, pursuant to the
   7|                         procedures outlined in Section 8.4., and there shall be sidewalk connections between the buildings. All utilities shall be placed underground.
   8|                   c) Building facades, materials and roof lines shall be consistent with the prevailing architecture and character of the Town and shall be compatible with other structures within the District.
   9|                   d) Flat roofs that are visible from the street level are prohibited unless approved by the Planning Board under the Development Plan Review.
  10|                   e) Facade and roof colors shall be appropriate to the area and consistent through the site except corporate signs
  11|                       approved under the Development Plan Review.
  12|                   f) The principal building(s) shall be connected to public water and sewer where readily available and accessible.
  13|                   g) Lighting, signage and architectural style shall be consistent with other uses in the District alternate design, which
  14|                       the Planning Board determines better serves the purposes of this Section is approved by the Planning Board under the Development Plan Review.
  15|                   h) LEED (Leadership in Energy and Environmental Design).
  16|                          1. LEED Certification should be encouraged to meet the best practicable level.
  17|                   i) Interior Streets, Drives, Walkways and Access.
  18|                          1. Site access shall be a divided way (one way in and one way out).
  19|                          2. Surfaces shall be pervious when possible and practical, shall be of material and appearance consistent
  20|                   j Parking and Loading Area. throughout the project, and approved by the Planning Board under the Development Plan Review.
  21|                           1. Parking shall be in the rear of building(s) and not be visible from the street line wherever possible otherwise
  22|                           approved by the Planning Board under the Development Plan Review.
  23|                           2. All loading docks shall be to the rear of the building(s) and not visible from the street.
  24|                           3. All paved areas shall be separated from the lot line setbacks by a four (4) foot landscaped area of indigenous materials so as to provide a buffer.
  25|                   k) Sidewalks.
  26|                           1. Sidewalks shall be provided from the street line, when applicable, and from the parking areas to building(s).
  27|                    1) Screening.
  28|                           1. Screening of the site shall be by a four (4) foot landscaped strip at the rear and side lot lines of materials
  29|                           approved by the Planning Board.
  30|                           2. Additional landscaping and screening may be required by the Planning Board during Development Plan Review
  31|                           where the Planning Board deems such additional screening appropriate in order to serve the purposes of this
  32|                    m) Landscaping.

  34|                           side of the principal building(s) and plantings of indigenous materials along the facades of the building(s) and between the building(s) if there is more than one principal building on site.
  35|                            2. Such landscape shall be a type and height that does not interfere with site lines of drivers.
  36|                            3. Natural features shall be retained to the extent possible.

  23|                   ARTICLE 8. To see if the Town will vote to amend the Zoning Bylaw by deleting current Section 4.6.5. Limitations Upon
  24|                   Use and replace with a new Section 4.6.5. Design Standards, as follows:
  25|                   4.6.5. DESIGN STANDARDS
  26|                   4.6.5.1. PURPOSES: The purposes of this Section are:
  27|                   a) To assure development which is compatible with prevailing architecture and character of the Town.
  28|                   b) To protect the environment.
  29|                   c) To enhance rather than detract from the quality and character of the Town.
  30|                    d) To preserve and enhance property values.
  31|                   4.6.5.2. STANDARDS: In addition to requirements contained elsewhere in this Bylaw the following design elements shall
  32|                   pertain in the Commercial District, permitted by the Zoning Board of Appeals (ZBA) and where applicable shall be
  33|                   reviewed and approved by the Planning Board in connection with the Development Plan Review under Section 8.4.
  34|                    a) Occupied Lot Area.
  35|                      1. The gross floor area of all buildings and structures on a lot shall occupy not more than forty (40%) percent of total lot area.
  36|                      2. The total area on any lot devoted to building, parking, outdoor storage and display and other paved hard surface
  37|                          areas may occupy up to eighty-five (85%) percent of the total lot area.
  38|                    b) Building Location and Utilities.
  39|                      1. The Building front shall face the street on which the lot obtains its frontage.
  40|                      2. If there is more than one building on the site, the siting shall be reviewed through the Development Plan Review
  41|                       3. All utilities shall be placed underground. (DPR) pursuant to the procedures outlined in Section 8.4.
  42|                   c) Building facades, materials and roof lines shall be reviewed under the Development Plan Review (DPR) regarding
  43|                    consistency and compatibility with other structures within the District.
  44|                   d) Flat roofs that are visible from the street level are allowed with a façade approved under the Development Plan
  45|                   Review (DPR) and permitted by the Special Permitting Authority.
  46|                   e) Façade, roof colors and signs shall be reviewed for consistency under the Development Plan Review (DPR).
  47|                   f) The principal building(s) shall be connected to public water and sewer where readily available and accessible.
  48|                   g) Lighting, signage and architectural style shall be consistent with other uses in the District and reviewed under the under the Development Plan Review (DPR).
  49|                   h) LEED (Leadership in Energy and Environmental Design)
  50|                      1. LEED Certification should be encouraged to meet the best practicable level.
  51|                                                                                            87

===PAGE 91=== (ocr, 50 lines)
   1|                       i) Interior Streets, Drives, Walkways and Access.
   2|                         1. Site access shall be a divided way (one way in and one way out) where and when appropriate, determined under
   3|                              the Development Plan Review (DPR).
   4|                         2. Surfaces shall be pervious when possible and practical excluding the required parking areas.
   5|                       j) Parking and Loading Area.
   6|                          1. Parking shall be in the rear or side of building(s) when possible. Parking will be reviewed under the Development Plan Review (DPR).
   7|                         2. All loading docks shall be to the rear of the building(s) and shall not be visible from the street.
   8|                         3. All paved areas shall be separated from the lot line setbacks by a four (4) foot landscaped area of indigenous materials.

  30|                    ARTICLE 10. To see if the Town will vote to amend the Zoning Bylaw Section 3.0. Establishment of Districts, 3.1. by
  31|                    adding a new section; "n) Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District" as follows:
  32|                    Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District PURPOSE
  33|                    The Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District has been established to encourage development that in itself is
  34|                    compatible and aligns with the character of the Town, created through amenities, a sense of community that is an
  35|                    appealing place to live and work ensuring the vision of Lunenburg as a New England Town. Further, by providing clear
  36|                    and precise guidelines as to the building and lot design that will revitalize the area and when possible, using sustainable building materials and design features that include indoor/outdoor conservation methods.
  37|                    LOCATION
  38|                    In Lunenburg from the Fitchburg line to the Leominster line including all of Industrial District along Summer Street in
  39|                    Lunenburg and all of the Commercial District alon Summer Street and Youngs Road in Lunenburg.
  40|                                                                                              89

===PAGE 93=== (ocr, 55 lines)
   1|                      OBJECTIVES
   2|                     a. Allowing a mix of uses in close proximity in the district within the development including residential, retail, office,
   3|                            and light industrial;
   4|                     b. Preserving and restoring a village style character to the designated overlay area;
   5|                      C.   Promoting a balance of land uses;
   6|                        Promoting the opportunity for people to work, meet, shop, and utilize services in the vicinity of their residences;
   7|                      e.   Providing opportunities for the development of variety of housing opportunities;
   8|                     f. Providing opportunities for a mixture of uses in the same building;
   9|                           Promoting a positive pedestrian environment in the district;
  10|                     h.    Facilitating integrated physical design;
  11|                      i.   Promoting a consistent level of design quality;
  12|                     j.  Encouraging the development of flexible space for small and emerging businesses;
  13|                      k.   Facilitating development proposals responsive to current and future market conditions;
  14|                     I.    Encouraging the development of open spaces and parks within the district to accommodate workers, residents,
  15|                           pedestrians, and shoppers.
  16|                     PERMIT GRANTING AUTHORITY
  17|                     The Zoning Board of Appeals (ZBA) is hereby the permit granting authority pertaining to the Commercial District and
  18|                     where applicable projects proposed within the Overlay District shall be reviewed under Section 8.4 Development Plan
  19|                     Review. The Planning Board is hereby the permit granting authority pertaining to the Industrial District where
  20|                     applicable projects proposed within the Overlay District shall be reviewed under Section 8.4, Development Plan Review.
  21|                     PERMITTED USES
  22|                     The following uses are permitted within the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District.
  23|                     (a) Any use permitted by right or special permit in the underlying zone.
  24|                    (b) Mixed Use Development - is a development of a tract of land, building, or structure with two (2) or more different
  25|                           uses such as, but not limited to, residential, office, retail, institutional, or entertainment. Residential uses shall be
  26|                           allowed as part of a mixed use project.
  27|                     DIMENSIONAL STANDARDS
  28|                     These standards herein are hereby established as such to achieve a village style design for the Summer Street
  29|                     Revitalization Overlay District.
  30|                    (a) Occupied Lot Area:
  31|                          1.    The gross floor area of all buildings and structures on a lot shall not occupy more than forty (40%) percent of
  32|                                the total lot area.
  33|                           2. The total area on any lot devoted to building, parking, outdoor storage, and display and other hard surface
  34|                                areas may occupy up to eighty-five (85%) percent of the total lot area.
  35|                     (b) Maximum Height: No more than 55 feet measured from ground level.
  36|                    (C) Setbacks:                Front - 20 feet, Side - 15 feet, Rear - 20 feet
  37|                           A landscape plan shall be required for screening and buffering purposes for setback areas.
  38|                    (d) Minimum Lot Size: Twenty Thousand (20,000) square feet
  39|                    (e) Minimum Frontage: Fifty (50) feet
  40|                    (f) Minimum Open Space: All projects within the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District shall have at least ten
  41|                          (10%) of the total site area devoted to Open Space; required setbacks shall be considered as part of the total area
  42|                          required for Open Space. The required Open Space shall not be used for parking or loading purposes and shall be
  43|                           open and unobstructed to the sky, items such as benches, walkways, planters, landscaping, kiosks, gazebos and
  44|                           similar structures shall not be considered obstructions.
  45|                    (g) Mixed Use Developments: that propose to have retail and residential uses within the same building on lots that
  46|                           meet the minimum dimensional requirements established herein, shall be allowed only in a two story building
  47|                           permitted by the Special Permitting Authority.
  48|                    (h) Bonus Density: A Mix Use Development with in the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District which provides
  49|                          at least ten (10%) percent of its residential units be made affordable to low to moderate income persons as defined
  50|                           by the Executive Office of Housing and Economic Development (EOHED).
  51|                    ADDTIONAL STANDARDS
  52|                     As well as the standards required under Section 8.4 Development Plan Review, these additional standards are
  53|                    established for the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District.
  54|                    a) Building - Location & Facade
  55|                                                                                              90

===PAGE 95=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|                      PURPOSE Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District
   2|                      The Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District has been established to encourage development that in itself is
   3|                      compatible and aligns with the character of the Town, created through amenities, a sense of community that is an
   4|                      appealing place to live and work ensuring the vision of Lunenburg as a New England Town. Further, by providing clear
   5|                     LOCATION sustainable building materials and design features that include indoor/outdoor conservation methods. and precise guidelines as to the building and lot design that will revitalize the area and when possible, using
   6|                     OBJECTIVES Lunenburg and all of the Commercial District alon Summer Street and Youngs Road in Lunenburg. In Lunenburg from the Fitchburg line to the Leominster line including all of Industrial District along Summer Street in
   7|                     m. Allowing a mix of uses in close proximity in the district within the development including residential, retail, office, and light industrial;
   8|                     n. Preserving and restoring a village style character to the designated overlay area; o. Promoting a balance of land uses;
   9|                    p. Promoting the opportunity for people to work, meet, shop, and utilize services in the vicinity of their residences; r. q. Providing opportunities for the development of variety of housing opportunities;
  10|                    S.    Providing opportunities for a mixture of uses in the same building;
  11|                    t.   Promoting a positive pedestrian environment in the district;
  12|                    U.   Facilitating integrated physical design;
  13|                    V.    Promoting a consistent level of design quality;
  14|                   W.    Encouraging the development of flexible space for small and emerging businesses;
  15|                   x.    Facilitating development proposals responsive to current and future market conditions;
  16|                       Encouraging the development of open spaces and parks within the district to accommodate workers, residents, pedestrians, and shoppers.
  17|                   PERMIT GRANTING AUTHORITY
  18|                   The Zoning Board of Appeals (ZBA) is hereby the review authority pertaining to the Commercial District and where
  19|                   applicable projects proposed within the Overlay District shall be reviewed under Section 8.4., Development Plan
  20|                   Review. The Planning Board is hereby the permit granting authority pertaining to the Industrial District where
  21|                  PERMITTED USES applicable projects proposed within the Overlay District shall be reviewed under Section 8.4., Development Plan
  22|                  The following uses are permitted within the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District. (c) Any use permitted by right or special permit in the underlying zone.
  23|                  (d) Mixed Use Development - is a development of a tract of land, building, or structure with two (2) or more different
  24|                  DIMENSIONAL STANDARDS uses such as, but not limited to, residential, office, retail, institutional, or entertainment. Residential uses shall be allowed as part of a mixed use project.
  25|                  These standards herein are hereby established as such to achieve a village style design for the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District.
  26|                  (i) Occupied Lot Area:
  27|                       3. The total area on any lot devoted to building, parking, outdoor storage, and display and other hard surface areas may occupy up to eighty-five (85%) percent of the total lot area.
  28|                 (K) Setbacks: tHeN more than 55 feet measured from ground level.
  29|                                              Front - 20 feet, Side - 15 feet, Rear - 20 feet
  30|                 ) Minimum Lot Size: Twenty Thousand (20,000) square feet A landscape plan shall be required for screening and buffering purposes for setback areas.
  31|                (m) Minimum Frontage: Fifty (50) feet
  32|                (n) Minimum Open Space: All projects within the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District shall have at least ten
  33|                      (10%) of the total site area devoted to Open Space; required setbacks shall be considered as part of the total area
  34|                      required for Open Space. The required Open Space shall not be used for parking or loading purposes and shall be
  35|                      open and unobstructed to the sky, items such as benches, walkways, planters, landscaping, kiosks, gazebos and similar structures shall not be considered obstructions.
  36|                                                                                            92

===PAGE 96=== (ocr, 42 lines)
   1|                        (o) Mixed Use Developments: that propose to have retail and residential uses within the same building on lots that
   2|                              meet the minimum dimensional requirements established herein, shall be allowed only in a two story building.
   3|                        (P) A Mix Use Development within the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District must provide at least ten (10%)
   4|                              percent of its residential units be made affordable to low to moderate income persons as defined by the Executive Office of Housing and Economic Development (EOHED).
   5|                        ADDTIONAL STANDARDS
   6|                        As well as the standards required under Section 8.4 Development Plan Review, these additional standards are established for the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District.
   7|                            Building - Location & Facade
   8|                             7. The Building front shall face the street on which the lot obtains its frontage.
   9|                             8. If there is more than one building on the site, the siting shall be approved by the Special Permitting Authority
  10|                                   pursuant to the procedures outlined in Section 8.4, and there shall be sidewalk connections between buildings.
  11|                             9.
  12|                                Flat roofs that are visible from the street level are prohibited unless an appropriate façade is included in the design.
  13|                            10. Roof colors shall be appropriate to the area and consistent through the site except signs approved under the Development Plan Review.
  14|                            11. The principal building(s) shall be connected to public water and sewer where readily available and accessible.
  15|                            12. Lighting, signage, and architectural style shall be consistent with other uses in the District and reviewed under the development Plan Review (DPR).
  16|                       f) Parking, Loading, and Interior Streets
  17|                            6. Parking Lots shall be located at the rear of or the side of buildings wherever feasible or practical.
  18|                            7. Parking lot layout shall take into consideration pedestrian circulation. Pedestrian crosswalks shall be provided, where necessary and appropriate.
  19|                            8. Turning radius of emergency response vehicles shall be considered within the design of the project.
  20|                            9. All loading docks shall be at the rear of building(s) and not visible from the street.
  21|                            10. All paved areas shall be separated from the lot line setback by a four (4) foot landscaped area of indigenous materials.
  22|                      g) Landscaping and Screening
  23|                           6. There shall be a minimum of a four (4) foot landscaped area along the street frontage and along the front and
  24|                                 side of the principal building(s) and plantings of indigenous material along the façade of the building(s) and between the building(s) if there is more than one building on site.
  25|                           8. Natural features shall be retained wherever feasible. 7. Such landscaping does not interfere with sight lines of drivers.
  26|                           9.    Screening of the site shall be by a four (4) foot landscaped strip at the rear and side lot lines
  27|                          10. Additional landscaping and screening may be required where it is deemed such appropriate measures are in • order.
  28|                     h) Streetscape
  29|                           6.   Light fixtures shall be designed to appropriately blend within the District and be of number and height that
  30|                               Lighting must also be placed on the side and rear of the building. grants plentiful lighting. Such lighting shall shine downward as to not affect adjacent properties.
  31|                           8. Outdoor tables, benches, and bicycle racks shall be consistent with the principal use(s) of the site and consistent with the character of the surrounding uses within the District.
  32|                           9. Trash receptacles must be of a size that provides proper usage.
  33|                    WAIVER 10. Sidewalks shall be provided from the street line, when applicable, and from the parking areas to building(s).
  34|                    The review authority may waive any of the standards within this Section provided that such waiver will not substantially derogate from the village style design standard established herein.
  35|                    COMMONLY HELD LOTS
  36|                    Any lot that is commonly held in ownership with an adjacent lot in this district may be treated as a single lot in
  37|                    accordance with this Section, provided that the total area of such lots is at least 20,000 square feet in area, the lots have
  38|                    a combined contiguous frontage of at least fifty (50) feet, and vacant of structures, parking facilities, or accessory uses. CONFLICT WITH OTHER LAWS
  39|                    All development activities within the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District shall comply with applicable laws,
  40|                   regulations, and standards of the Town, except that in the event of a conflict between this bylaw and any such laws and
  41|                   regulations, the provisions of this Bylaw shall control, provided that they are consistent with state and federal law.
  42|                                                                                              93

===PAGE 97=== (ocr, 4 lines)
   1|                          SEVERABILITY
   2|                          If any section or provision of this bylaw is found by a court of competent jurisdiction to be invalid, such invalidity shall not affect the validity of any other section or provision of this Bylaw.
